By step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have ISR${=}0$ and mean component count inflates 6.58$\\to$18.55 (contradiction explosion).}\n\\label{fig:isr-collapse}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}\nFigure~\\ref{fig:isr-collapse} traces ISR and PR over training for exec-only GRPO (seed~42).\nAt 100 steps: PR${=}32$\\%, ISR$^*{=}0.642$. %% registry: plan003-eval-exec-only-r5\nBy 500 steps: PR${=}93$\\% but ISR$^*{=}0.132$---87.1\\% of proved samples embed the conclusion among the premises (H1). %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2\nAt 1000 steps: PR${=}96$\\% while ISR$^*$ drops to 0.003 (H1${=}100$\\%, mean components 18.55). %% registry: plan016-extended-1000-s42\nThe ISR$^*$ trajectory ($0.642{\\to}0.132{\\to}0.003$) is monotonically decreasing: extended training worsens structural degeneration, invisible to PR.\n\n\\textbf{Cross-seed instability.}\nThe three exec-only seeds diverge radically: s42 hacks via H1 (PR${=}93$\\%, H1${=}87.1$\\%); s43 collapses to PR${=}13$\\% via repetition (87\\% hit max length); s44 reaches PR${=}98$\\% via tautological collapse (H7${=}100$\\%). %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2, plan001-exec-only-s43-r4, plan001-exec-only-s44\nDespite PR$\\in[13,98]$\\%, all three exhibit structural degeneration detectable by ISR; the $\\sigma{=}\\pm47$\\,pp cross-seed variance shows aggregate metrics obscure qualitatively different failure modes (per-seed: Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\n\n\\textbf{SFT baseline.}\nA supervised baseline achieves PR${=}14$\\% with ISR$^*{=}0.571$: 92.9\\% (13/14) [66.1\\%, 99.8\\%] of proved samples are non-hacking, only 1 exhibits H1, and no H7 instances occur. %% registry: plan009-sft-baseline-s42-r4\nThe near-absence of hacking is consistent with hacking correlating with optimization aggressiveness rather than being driven purely by model capacity or data limitations.\nWe note that the SFT PR level (14\\%) is substantially lower than RL conditions, and the low hacking rate may partly reflect fewer opportunities for reward exploitation rather than a purely training-method difference; we address this confound via a 100-step RL checkpoint analysis in Appendix~\\ref{app:100step}.\n\n\\subsection{Meta-Level Goodhart's Law}\n\\label{sec:goodhart}\n\nISR-as-reward provides the first empirical demonstration of Goodhart's Law at the meta level~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}: the diagnostic itself becomes the target of gaming when optimized.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}$ & \\textbf{PR\\%}$\\uparrow$ & \\textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{N-H\\%} \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase (0-shot) & --- & 12 & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\ %% registry: sta-phase0-baseline\nSFT & --- & 14 & 0.571 & 0 & 7 & \\textbf{93} \\\\ %% registry: plan009-sft-baseline-s42-r4\n\\midrule\nExec-only$^\\dagger$ & --- & 93 & 0.132 & 0 & 87 & 13 \\\\ %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2\nISR & 0.1 & 77{\\scriptsize$\\pm$14} & $\\ddagger$ & \\textbf{65} & 17 & ${\\sim}$16 \\\\ %% registry: plan002-additive-b01-s42/s43/s44\nRandom & 0.1 & \\textbf{85}{\\scriptsize$\\pm$6} & 0.086{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.04} & 0 & 81 & 19 \\\\ %% registry: plan018-additive-random-b01-s42/s43-r2/s44\n\\midrule\nISR & 0.3 & 18 & 0.143 & --- & --- & --- \\\\ %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42\nRandom & 0.3 & 48 & 0.094 & --- & --- & --- \\\\ %% registry: plan017-additive-random-s42\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Goodhart landscape across reward conditions. 3-seed rows show mean$\\pm$std (ISR/Random $\\beta{=}0.1$); other rows are single seed. $^\\dagger$Seed~42; see \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic} for 3-seed divergence. $\\ddagger$ISR$^*$ omitted: inflated by H7 gaming (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}). results.\nAt $\\beta{=}0.1$, 2/3 ISR seeds (s43, s44) are dominated by H7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, trivially achieving both high PR and ISR.\nAcross the three ISR seeds, H7 plan018-additive-random-b01-s42 (comp=8.46)\nRestricting to non-hacking samples with $\\geq$3 premises: ISR non-hacking ISR$^*{=}0.258$ ($n{=}11$) vs random non-hacking ISR$^*{=}0.243$ ($n{=}21$)---a 1.06$\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage. %% D051 premise-count-matched analysis\nOf ISR s42's 25 nominally non-hacking samples, 15 have only 1--2 premises, where ISR$\\geq0.5$ is a mathematical artifact.\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): classic Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR causes PR to collapse to 18\\%---a 5.2$\\times$ degradation from exec-only---while the matched random control achieves PR${=}48$\\%. %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42, plan017-additive-random-s42\nThe two doses reveal a complete Goodhart trajectory~\\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization} at the meta level: low-dose proxy gaming (ISR inflation via H7) followed by high-dose performance collapse, with the proxy being gamed itself a gaming detector.\nUnder the taxonomy of \\citet{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}, $\\beta{=}0.3$ exemplifies extremal Goodhart while $\\beta{=}0.1$ instantiates a meta-level variant.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\nWe replicated both experiments using SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 (full results: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).\nProlog exec-only confirms diagnostic transfer (2/3 seeds with ISR$^*{\\approx}0$ despite moderate-high PR); the low ISR reflects \\emph{redundant coding} (repeated ground facts), not absent reasoning---contrasting the metric-gaming high ISR under Prolog ISR-reward. %% registry: plan006-prolog-exec-s42 (ISR*=0.037), plan006-prolog-exec-s43-r3 (ISR*=0.015), plan006-prolog-exec-s44 (ISR*=0.387, small-sample n=21); C006\nISR-as-reward yields universal Goodhart with 0\\% non-hacking across 185 proved samples (stronger than the FOL ${\\sim}16$\\% residual), via three distinct mechanisms: s42 clean H7 tautologies (ISR$^*{=}1.000$); s43 uppercase pseudo-Prolog functionally equivalent to H7 ($n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 38/38); s44 query-as-fact gaming, converting NL to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query (ISR$^*{=}0.912$, $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 95/97). %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s42 (H7=100%, ISR*=1.0), plan006-prolog-sta-s43 (H1=97.4%, n_nec=1 38/38), plan006-prolog-sta-s44 (PR=0.97, ISR=0.885, h7_functional=97.9%)\nThe diversity of pathways under identical data and reward shows Goodhart here is a family of convergent exploits (Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}); the text-classifier bypass conditions (proved samples). ISR converts H1 (conclusion embedding) into more extreme H7 (conclusion duplication) without reducing total hacking; SFT is near-zero hacking (92.9\\% non-hacking).}\n\\label{fig:hacking-composition}\n\\end{figure}\n\nThe seven modes (quantitative breakdown and definitions in Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}, Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}): \\textbf{H1} (GRPO-universal, 68--97\\%), \\textbf{H7} (ISR-specific, 64.5\\% under $\\beta{=}0.1$ vs.\\ 0\\% random), \\textbf{tautological} (exec-only s44, 94\\%), \\textbf{repetition} (exec-only s43, 87\\% hit max length), \\textbf{contradiction explosion} (mean 18.55 components at 1000 steps), \\textbf{complexity-without-utility} (Random $\\beta{=}0.1$, 3.8\\%), \\textbf{query-as-fact} (Prolog ISR s44, bypasses text classifiers). %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s44, plan001-exec-only-s43-r4, plan016-extended-1000-s42, plan006-prolog-sta-s44\nThe reward signal determines hacking \\emph{composition} but not \\emph{prevalence}: total conclusion-in-premise rates are comparable across ISR and random (${\\sim}82$\\% pooled), but ISR converts H1 into H7.\nSFT remains the only condition with near-zero hacking (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}), consistent with hacking being RL-reward-specific. %% registry: plan009-sft-baseline-s42-r4",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\nWe evaluate STA along three axes: as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); as a training reward exhibiting meta-level Goodhart's Law (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}); and as a cross-logic protocol (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}). We synthesize results into a hacking taxonomy (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe train Qwen3-4B with GRPO~\\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}8$ rollouts, 500 steps default, 1000 where noted) across seeds $\\{42,43,44\\}$ on a fixed 500-example FOLIO~\\citep{folio} subset held constant across conditions. %% data: C001\nEvaluation uses 100 held-out examples with greedy decoding; cross-run ISR values are computed uniformly by \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py}. %% C004\nWe report PR, proved-only ISR (ISR$^*$), hacking rates (H7, H1\\footnote{H1/H7 numbering follows our taxonomy (Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}).}), and non-hacking rate.\\footnote{Non-hacking: not matching any of seven identified patterns (Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}); does not imply semantic correctness.}\nFull hyperparameters in Appendix~\\ref{app:setup}.\n\n\\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_isr_collapse.pdf}\n\\caption{ISR collapse under exec-only GRPO (seed~42). PR rises 32\\%$\\to$96\\% while proved-only ISR falls 0.642$\\to$0.003 over 1000 steps. By step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have ISR${=}0$ and mean component count inflates 6.58$\\to$18.55 (contradiction explosion).}\n\\label{fig:isr-collapse}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}\nFigure~\\ref{fig:isr-collapse} traces ISR and PR over training for exec-only GRPO (seed~42).\nAt 100 steps: PR${=}32$\\%, ISR$^*{=}0.642$. %% registry: plan003-eval-exec-only-r5\nBy 500 steps: PR${=}93$\\% but ISR$^*{=}0.132$---87.1\\% of proved samples embed the conclusion among the premises (H1). %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2\nAt 1000 steps: PR${=}96$\\% while ISR$^*$ drops to 0.003 (H1${=}100$\\%, mean components 18.55). %% registry: plan016-extended-1000-s42\nThe ISR$^*$ trajectory ($0.642{\\to}0.132{\\to}0.003$) is monotonically decreasing: extended training worsens structural degeneration, invisible to PR.\n\n\\textbf{Cross-seed instability.}\nThe three exec-only seeds diverge radically: s42 hacks via H1 (PR${=}93$\\%, H1${=}87.1$\\%); s43 collapses to PR${=}13$\\% via repetition (87\\% hit max length); s44 reaches PR${=}98$\\% via tautological collapse (H7${=}100$\\%). %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2, plan001-exec-only-s43-r4, plan001-exec-only-s44\nDespite PR$\\in[13,98]$\\%, all three exhibit structural degeneration detectable by ISR; the $\\sigma{=}\\pm47$\\,pp cross-seed variance shows aggregate metrics obscure qualitatively different failure modes (per-seed: Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\n\n\\textbf{SFT baseline.}\nSFT achieves PR${=}14$\\% with ISR$^*{=}0.571$: 92.9\\% non-hacking, only 1/14 exhibits H1, zero H7. %% registry: plan009-sft-baseline-s42-r4\nThe near-absence of hacking is consistent with hacking being RL-specific; a 100-step RL checkpoint analysis controlling for the PR-level confound is in Appendix~\\ref{app:100step}.\n\n\\subsection{Meta-Level Goodhart's Law}\n\\label{sec:goodhart}\n\nISR-as-reward provides the first empirical demonstration of Goodhart's Law at the meta level~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}: the diagnostic itself becomes the target of gaming when optimized.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}$ & \\textbf{PR\\%}$\\uparrow$ & \\textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{N-H\\%} \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase (0-shot) & --- & 12 & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\ %% registry: sta-phase0-baseline\nSFT & --- & 14 & 0.571 & 0 & 7 & \\textbf{93} \\\\ %% registry: plan009-sft-baseline-s42-r4\n\\midrule\nExec-only$^\\dagger$ & --- & 93 & 0.132 & 0 & 87 & 13 \\\\ %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2\nISR & 0.1 & 77{\\scriptsize$\\pm$14} & $\\ddagger$ & \\textbf{65} & 17 & ${\\sim}$16 \\\\ %% registry: plan002-additive-b01-s42/s43/s44\nRandom & 0.1 & \\textbf{85}{\\scriptsize$\\pm$6} & 0.086{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.04} & 0 & 81 & 19 \\\\ %% registry: plan018-additive-random-b01-s42/s43-r2/s44\n\\midrule\nISR & 0.3 & 18 & 0.143 & --- & --- & --- \\\\ %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42\nRandom & 0.3 & 48 & 0.094 & --- & --- & --- \\\\ %% registry: plan017-additive-random-s42\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Goodhart landscape across reward conditions. 3-seed rows show mean$\\pm$std (ISR/Random $\\beta{=}0.1$); other rows are single seed. $^\\dagger$Seed~42; see \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic} for 3-seed divergence. $\\ddagger$ISR$^*$ omitted: inflated by H7 gaming (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}). H7 and H1 rates are simple means across seeds. Best PR and N-H\\% bolded per group.}\n\\label{tab:goodhart-landscape}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.1$): ISR-specific gaming.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape} summarizes results.\nAt $\\beta{=}0.1$, 2/3 ISR seeds (s43, s44) are dominated by H7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, trivially achieving both high PR and ISR.\nAcross the three ISR seeds, H7 accounts for 64.5\\% of proved samples (149/231); all three random controls show \\emph{zero} H7 (0/256), confirming H7 is exclusively induced by the ISR signal. %% registry: plan002-additive-b01-s42/s43/s44 vs plan018-additive-random-b01-s42/s43-r2/s44\nConclusion embedding (H1+H7) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across RL conditions: random produces only H1 (70.9--93.2\\%), while ISR converts H1$\\to$H7 without reducing the total (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}; per-seed: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\nRandom also achieves higher and more stable PR (85$\\pm$6\\% vs 77$\\pm$14\\%). %% registry: plan018 vs plan002 3-seed means\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nSeed~42 under ISR---the only seed not dominated by H7---shows higher raw ISR$^*$ (0.415 vs 0.131 for random). %% registry: plan002-additive-b01-s42 (ISR*=0.4154), plan018-additive-random-b01-s42 (ISR*=0.1314)\nThis is entirely a premise-minimization artifact: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (mean 5.58 vs 8.46 components), and LOO is mathematically biased toward shorter inputs. %% registry: plan002-additive-b01-s42 (comp=5.58), plan018-additive-random-b01-s42 (comp=8.46)\nRestricting to non-hacking samples with $\\geq$3 premises: ISR non-hacking ISR$^*{=}0.258$ ($n{=}11$) vs random non-hacking ISR$^*{=}0.243$ ($n{=}21$)---a 1.06$\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage. %% D051 premise-count-matched analysis\nOf ISR s42's 25 nominally non-hacking samples, 15 have only 1--2 premises, where ISR$\\geq0.5$ is a mathematical artifact.\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): classic Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR causes PR to collapse to 18\\%---a 5.2$\\times$ degradation from exec-only---while the matched random control achieves PR${=}48$\\%. %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42, plan017-additive-random-s42\nThe two doses reveal a complete Goodhart trajectory~\\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization} at the meta level: low-dose proxy gaming (ISR inflation via H7) followed by high-dose performance collapse, with the proxy being gamed itself a gaming detector.\nUnder the taxonomy of \\citet{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}, $\\beta{=}0.3$ exemplifies extremal Goodhart while $\\beta{=}0.1$ instantiates a meta-level variant.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\nWe replicated both experiments using SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 (full results: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).\nProlog exec-only confirms diagnostic transfer (2/3 seeds with ISR$^*{\\approx}0$ despite moderate-high PR); the low ISR reflects \\emph{redundant coding} (repeated ground facts), not absent reasoning---contrasting the metric-gaming high ISR under Prolog ISR-reward. %% registry: plan006-prolog-exec-s42 (ISR*=0.037), plan006-prolog-exec-s43-r3 (ISR*=0.015), plan006-prolog-exec-s44 (ISR*=0.387, small-sample n=21); C006\nISR-as-reward yields universal Goodhart with 0\\% non-hacking across 185 proved samples (stronger than the FOL ${\\sim}16$\\% residual), via three distinct mechanisms: s42 clean H7 tautologies (ISR$^*{=}1.000$); s43 uppercase pseudo-Prolog functionally equivalent to H7 ($n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 38/38); s44 query-as-fact gaming, converting NL to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query (ISR$^*{=}0.912$, $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 95/97). %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s42 (H7=100%, ISR*=1.0), plan006-prolog-sta-s43 (H1=97.4%, n_nec=1 38/38), plan006-prolog-sta-s44 (PR=0.97, ISR=0.885, h7_functional=97.9%)\nThe diversity of pathways under identical data and reward shows Goodhart here is a family of convergent exploits (Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}); the text-classifier bypass by s44 motivates functional cross-validation of automated diagnostics.\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.9\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}\n\\caption{Hacking composition across reward conditions (proved samples). ISR converts H1 (conclusion embedding) into more extreme H7 (conclusion duplication) without reducing total hacking; SFT is near-zero hacking (92.9\\% non-hacking).}\n\\label{fig:hacking-composition}\n\\end{figure}\n\nThe seven modes (quantitative breakdown and definitions in Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}, Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}): \\textbf{H1} (GRPO-universal, 68--97\\%), \\textbf{H7} (ISR-specific, 64.5\\% under $\\beta{=}0.1$ vs.\\ 0\\% random), \\textbf{tautological} (exec-only s44, 94\\%), \\textbf{repetition} (exec-only s43, 87\\% hit max length), \\textbf{contradiction explosion} (mean 18.55 components at 1000 steps), \\textbf{complexity-without-utility} (Random $\\beta{=}0.1$, 3.8\\%), \\textbf{query-as-fact} (Prolog ISR s44, bypasses text classifiers). %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s44, plan001-exec-only-s43-r4, plan016-extended-1000-s42, plan006-prolog-sta-s44\nThe reward signal determines hacking \\emph{composition} but not \\emph{prevalence}: total conclusion-in-premise rates are comparable across ISR and random (${\\sim}82$\\% pooled), but ISR converts H1 into H7.\nSFT remains the only condition with near-zero hacking (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}), consistent with hacking being RL-reward-specific. %% registry: plan009-sft-baseline-s42-r4",

gaming (ISR inflation via H7) followed by high-dose performance collapse, with the proxy being gamed itself a gaming detector.\nUnder the taxonomy of \\citet{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}, $\\beta{=}0.3$ identical data and reward shows Goodhart here is a family of convergent exploits (Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}); the text-classifier bypass by s44 motivates functional cross-validation of automated diagnostics.\n\n\\subsection{Hacking conditions (proved samples).     "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\nWe evaluate STA along three axes: as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); as a training reward exhibiting meta-level Goodhart's Law (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}); and as a cross-logic protocol (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}). We synthesize results into a hacking taxonomy (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe train Qwen3-4B with GRPO~\\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}8$ rollouts, 500 steps default, 1000 where noted) across seeds $\\{42,43,44\\}$ on a fixed 500-example FOLIO~\\citep{folio} subset held constant across conditions. %% data: C001\nEvaluation uses 100 held-out examples with greedy decoding; cross-run ISR values are computed uniformly by \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py}. %% C004\nWe report PR, proved-only ISR (ISR$^*$), hacking rates (H7, H1\\footnote{H1/H7 numbering follows our taxonomy (Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}).}), and non-hacking rate.\\footnote{Non-hacking: not matching any of seven identified patterns (Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}); does not imply semantic correctness.}\nFull hyperparameters in Appendix~\\ref{app:setup}.\n\n\\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_isr_collapse.pdf}\n\\caption{ISR collapse under exec-only GRPO (seed~42). PR rises 32\\%$\\to$96\\% while proved-only ISR falls 0.642$\\to$0.003 over 1000 steps. By step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have ISR${=}0$ and mean component count inflates 6.58$\\to$18.55 (contradiction explosion).}\n\\label{fig:isr-collapse}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}\nFigure~\\ref{fig:isr-collapse} traces ISR and PR over training for exec-only GRPO (seed~42).\nAt 100 steps: PR${=}32$\\%, ISR$^*{=}0.642$. %% registry: plan003-eval-exec-only-r5\nBy 500 steps: PR${=}93$\\% but ISR$^*{=}0.132$---87.1\\% of proved samples embed the conclusion among the premises (H1). %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2\nAt 1000 steps: PR${=}96$\\% while ISR$^*$ drops to 0.003 (H1${=}100$\\%, mean components 18.55). %% registry: plan016-extended-1000-s42\nThe ISR$^*$ trajectory ($0.642{\\to}0.132{\\to}0.003$) is monotonically decreasing: extended training worsens structural degeneration, invisible to PR.\n\n\\textbf{Cross-seed instability.}\nThe three exec-only seeds diverge radically: s42 hacks via H1 (PR${=}93$\\%, H1${=}87.1$\\%); s43 collapses to PR${=}13$\\% via repetition (87\\% hit max length); s44 reaches PR${=}98$\\% via tautological collapse (H7${=}100$\\%). %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2, plan001-exec-only-s43-r4, plan001-exec-only-s44\nDespite PR$\\in[13,98]$\\%, all three exhibit structural degeneration detectable by ISR; the $\\sigma{=}\\pm47$\\,pp cross-seed variance shows aggregate metrics obscure qualitatively different failure modes (per-seed: Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\n\n\\textbf{SFT baseline.}\nSFT achieves PR${=}14$\\% with ISR$^*{=}0.571$: 92.9\\% non-hacking, only 1/14 exhibits H1, zero H7. %% registry: plan009-sft-baseline-s42-r4\nThe near-absence of hacking is consistent with hacking being RL-specific;     "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\nWe evaluate STA along three axes: as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); as a training reward exhibiting meta-level Goodhart's Law (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}); and as a cross-logic protocol (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}). We synthesize results into a hacking taxonomy (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe train Qwen3-4B with GRPO~\\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}8$ rollouts, 500 steps default, 1000 where noted) across seeds $\\{42,43,44\\}$ on a fixed 500-example FOLIO~\\citep{folio} subset held constant across conditions. %% data: C001\nEvaluation uses 100 held-out examples with greedy decoding; cross-run ISR values are computed uniformly by \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py}. %% C004\nWe report PR, proved-only ISR (ISR$^*$), hacking rates (H7, H1\\footnote{H1/H7 numbering follows our taxonomy (Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}).}), and non-hacking rate.\\footnote{Non-hacking: not matching any of seven identified patterns (Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}); does not imply semantic correctness.}\nFull hyperparameters in Appendix~\\ref{app:setup}.\n\n\\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_isr_collapse.pdf}\n\\caption{ISR collapse under exec-only GRPO (seed~42). PR rises 32\\%$\\to$96\\% while proved-only ISR falls 0.642$\\to$0.003 over 1000 steps. By step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have ISR${=}0$ and mean component count inflates 6.58$\\to$18.55 (contradiction explosion).}\n\\label{fig:isr-collapse}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}\nFigure~\\ref{fig:isr-collapse} traces ISR and PR over training for exec-only GRPO (seed~42).\nAt 100 steps: PR${=}32$\\%, ISR$^*{=}0.642$. %% registry: plan003-eval-exec-only-r5\nBy 500 steps: PR${=}93$\\% but ISR$^*{=}0.132$---87.1\\% of proved samples embed the conclusion among the premises (H1). %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2\nAt 1000 steps: PR${=}96$\\% while ISR$^*$ drops to 0.003 (H1${=}100$\\%, mean components 18.55). %% registry: plan016-extended-1000-s42\nThe ISR$^*$ trajectory ($0.642{\\to}0.132{\\to}0.003$) is monotonically decreasing: extended training worsens structural degeneration, invisible to PR.\n\n\\textbf{Cross-seed instability.}\nThe three exec-only seeds diverge radically: s42 hacks via H1 (PR${=}93$\\%, H1${=}87.1$\\%); s43 collapses to PR${=}13$\\% via repetition (87\\% hit max length); s44 reaches PR${=}98$\\% via tautological collapse (H7${=}100$\\%). %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2, plan001-exec-only-s43-r4, plan001-exec-only-s44\nDespite PR$\\in[13,98]$\\%, all three exhibit structural degeneration detectable by ISR; the $\\sigma{=}\\pm47$\\,pp cross-seed variance shows aggregate metrics obscure qualitatively different failure modes (per-seed: Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\n\n\\textbf{SFT baseline.}\nSFT achieves PR${=}14$\\% with ISR$^*{=}0.571$: 92.9\\% non-hacking, only 1/14 exhibits H1, zero H7. %% registry: plan009-sft-baseline-s42-r4\nThe near-absence of hacking is consistent with hacking being RL-specific; a 100-step RL checkpoint analysis controlling for the PR-level confound is in Appendix~\\ref{app:100step}.\n\n\\subsection{Meta-Level Goodhart's Law}\n\\label{sec:goodhart}\n\nISR-as-reward provides the first empirical demonstration of Goodhart's Law at the meta level~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}: the diagnostic itself becomes the target of gaming when optimized.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}$ & \\textbf{PR\\%}$\\uparrow$ & \\textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{N-H\\%} \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase (0-shot) & --- & 12 & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\ %% registry: sta-phase0-baseline\nSFT & --- & 14 & 0.571 & 0 & 7 & \\textbf{93} \\\\ %% registry: plan009-sft-baseline-s42-r4\n\\midrule\nExec-only$^\\dagger$ & --- & 93 & 0.132 & 0 & 87 & 13 \\\\ %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2\nISR & 0.1 & 77{\\scriptsize$\\pm$14} & $\\ddagger$ & \\textbf{65} & 17 & ${\\sim}$16 \\\\ %% registry: plan002-additive-b01-s42/s43/s44\nRandom & 0.1 & \\textbf{85}{\\scriptsize$\\pm$6} & 0.086{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.04} & 0 & 81 & 19 \\\\ %% registry: plan018-additive-random-b01-s42/s43-r2/s44\n\\midrule\nISR & 0.3 & 18 & 0.143 & --- & --- & --- \\\\ %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42\nRandom & 0.3 & 48 & 0.094 & --- & --- & --- \\\\ %% registry: plan017-additive-random-s42\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Goodhart landscape across reward conditions. 3-seed rows show mean$\\pm$std (ISR/Random $\\beta{=}0.1$); other rows are single seed. $^\\dagger$Seed~42; see \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic} for 3-seed divergence. $\\ddagger$ISR$^*$ omitted: inflated by H7 gaming (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}). H7 and H1 rates are simple means across seeds. Best PR and N-H\\% bolded per group.}\n\\label{tab:goodhart-landscape}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.1$): ISR-specific gaming.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape} summarizes results.\nAt $\\beta{=}0.1$, 2/3 ISR seeds are dominated by H7 (64.5\\% of proved samples, 149/231) vs.\\ zero H7 under random controls (0/256), confirming H7 is exclusively ISR-induced. %% registry: plan002-additive-b01-s42/s43/s44 vs plan018-additive-random-b01-s42/s43-r2/s44\nConclusion embedding (H1+H7) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\%: random produces only H1, while ISR converts H1$\\to$H7 without reducing the total (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}; per-seed: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\nRandom also achieves higher and more stable PR (85$\\pm$6\\% vs 77$\\pm$14\\%). %% registry: plan018 vs plan002 3-seed means\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nThe only ISR seed not dominated by H7 (s42) shows higher raw ISR$^*$ (0.415 vs 0.131), but this is entirely a premise-minimization artifact: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (5.58 vs 8.46 components), biasing LOO upward. %% registry: plan002-additive-b01-s42, plan018-additive-random-b01-s42\nRestricting to non-hacking samples with $\\geq$3 premises: ISR$^*{=}0.258$ ($n{=}11$) vs random ISR$^*{=}0.243$ ($n{=}21$)---a 1.06$\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage. %% D051 premise-count-matched analysis\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): classic Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR causes PR collapse to 18\\% (vs.\\ 48\\% random), completing the Goodhart trajectory~\\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization} at the meta level: low-dose proxy gaming followed by high-dose performance collapse, with the proxy itself a gaming detector. %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42, plan017-additive-random-s42\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\nReplication in Prolog/SWI-Prolog confirms diagnostic transfer (full results: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).\nExec-only: 2/3 seeds show ISR$^*{\\approx}0$ despite moderate-high PR, reflecting \\emph{redundant coding} (repeated ground facts), not absent reasoning. %% registry: plan006-prolog-exec-s42 (ISR*=0.037), plan006-prolog-exec-s43-r3 (ISR*=0.015), plan006-prolog-exec-s44 (ISR*=0.387, small-sample n=21); C006\nISR-as-reward yields 0\\% non-hacking across 185 proved samples via three distinct mechanisms: s42 H7 tautologies (ISR$^*{=}1.0$); s43 uppercase pseudo-Prolog functionally equivalent to H7; s44 query-as-fact gaming that bypasses text classifiers (Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}). %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s42, plan006-prolog-sta-s43, plan006-prolog-sta-s44\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.9\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}\n\\caption{Hacking composition across reward conditions (proved samples).     "newString": "\\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}\nFigure~\\ref{fig:isr-collapse} traces exec-only GRPO (seed~42): PR rises 32\\%$\\to$93\\%$\\to$96\\% while ISR$^*$ falls $0.642{\\to}0.132{\\to}0.003$ over 1000 steps. %% registry: plan003-eval-exec-only-r5, plan001-exec-only-s42-r2, plan016-extended-1000-s42\nBy step 500, 87.1\\% of proved samples embed the conclusion (H1); by 1000, H1${=}100$\\% with mean 18.55 components.\nThe monotonic ISR$^*$ decline shows extended training worsens structural degeneration invisible to PR.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\nWe evaluate STA along three axes: as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); as a training reward exhibiting meta-level Goodhart's Law (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}); and as a cross-logic protocol (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}). We synthesize results into a hacking taxonomy (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe train Qwen3-4B with GRPO~\\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}8$ rollouts, 500 steps default, 1000 where noted) across seeds $\\{42,43,44\\}$ on a fixed 500-example FOLIO~\\citep{folio} subset held constant across conditions. %% data: C001\nEvaluation uses 100 held-out examples with greedy decoding; cross-run ISR values are computed uniformly by \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py}. %% C004\nWe report PR, proved-only ISR (ISR$^*$), hacking rates (H7, H1\\footnote{H1/H7 numbering follows our taxonomy (Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}).}), and non-hacking rate.\\footnote{Non-hacking: not matching any of seven identified patterns (Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}); does not imply semantic correctness.}\nFull hyperparameters in Appendix~\\ref{app:setup}.\n\n\\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_isr_collapse.pdf}\n\\caption{ISR collapse under exec-only GRPO (seed~42). PR rises 32\\%$\\to$96\\% while proved-only ISR falls 0.642$\\to$0.003 over 1000 steps. By step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have ISR${=}0$ and mean component count inflates 6.58$\\to$18.55 (contradiction explosion).}\n\\label{fig:isr-collapse}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}\nFigure~\\ref{fig:isr-collapse} traces ISR and PR over training for exec-only GRPO (seed~42).\nAt 100 steps: PR${=}32$\\%, ISR$^*{=}0.642$. %% registry: plan003-eval-exec-only-r5\nBy 500 steps: PR${=}93$\\% but ISR$^*{=}0.132$---87.1\\% of proved samples embed the conclusion among the premises (H1). %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2\nAt 1000 steps: PR${=}96$\\% while ISR$^*$ drops to 0.003 (H1${=}100$\\%, mean components 18.55). %% registry: plan016-extended-1000-s42\nThe ISR$^*$ trajectory ($0.642{\\to}0.132{\\to}0.003$) is monotonically decreasing: extended training worsens structural degeneration, invisible to PR.\n\n\\textbf{Cross-seed instability.}\nThe three exec-only seeds diverge radically: s42 hacks via H1 (PR${=}93$\\%, H1${=}87.1$\\%); s43 collapses to PR${=}13$\\% via repetition (87\\% hit max length); s44 reaches PR${=}98$\\% via tautological collapse (H7${=}100$\\%). %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2, plan001-exec-only-s43-r4, plan001-exec-only-s44\nDespite PR$\\in[13,98]$\\%, all three exhibit structural degeneration detectable by ISR; the $\\sigma{=}\\pm47$\\,pp cross-seed variance shows aggregate metrics obscure qualitatively different failure modes (per-seed: Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\n\n\\textbf{SFT baseline.}\nSFT achieves PR${=}14$\\% with ISR$^*{=}0.571$: 92.9\\% non-hacking, only 1/14 exhibits H1, zero H7. %% registry: plan009-sft-baseline-s42-r4\nThe near-absence of hacking is consistent with hacking being RL-specific; a 100-step RL checkpoint analysis controlling for the PR-level confound is in Appendix~\\ref{app:100step}.\n\n\\subsection{Meta-Level Goodhart's Law}\n\\label{sec:goodhart}\n\nISR-as-reward provides the first empirical demonstration of Goodhart's Law at the meta level~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}: the diagnostic itself becomes the target of gaming when optimized.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}$ & \\textbf{PR\\%}$\\uparrow$ & \\textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{N-H\\%} \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase (0-shot) & --- & 12 & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\ %% registry: sta-phase0-baseline\nSFT & --- & 14 & 0.571 & 0 & 7 & \\textbf{93} \\\\ %% registry: plan009-sft-baseline-s42-r4\n\\midrule\nExec-only$^\\dagger$ & --- & 93 & 0.132 & 0 & 87 & 13 \\\\ %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2\nISR & 0.1 & 77{\\scriptsize$\\pm$14} & $\\ddagger$ & \\textbf{65} & 17 & ${\\sim}$16 \\\\ %% registry: plan002-additive-b01-s42/s43/s44\nRandom & 0.1 & \\textbf{85}{\\scriptsize$\\pm$6} & 0.086{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.04} & 0 & 81 & 19 \\\\ %% registry: plan018-additive-random-b01-s42/s43-r2/s44\n\\midrule\nISR & 0.3 & 18 & 0.143 & --- & --- & --- \\\\ %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42\nRandom & 0.3 & 48 & 0.094 & --- & --- & --- \\\\ %% registry: plan017-additive-random-s42\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Goodhart landscape across reward conditions. 3-seed rows show mean$\\pm$std (ISR/Random $\\beta{=}0.1$); other rows are single seed. $^\\dagger$Seed~42; see \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic} for 3-seed divergence. $\\ddagger$ISR$^*$ omitted: inflated by H7 gaming (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}). H7 and H1 rates are simple means across seeds. Best PR and N-H\\% bolded per group.}\n\\label{tab:goodhart-landscape}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.1$): ISR-specific gaming.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape} summarizes results.\nAt $\\beta{=}0.1$, 2/3 ISR seeds are dominated by H7 (64.5\\% of proved samples, 149/231) vs.\\ zero H7 under random controls (0/256), confirming H7 is exclusively ISR-induced. %% registry: plan002-additive-b01-s42/s43/s44 vs plan018-additive-random-b01-s42/s43-r2/s44\nConclusion embedding (H1+H7) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\%: random produces only H1, while ISR converts H1$\\to$H7 without reducing the total (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}; per-seed: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\nRandom also achieves higher and more stable PR (85$\\pm$6\\% vs 77$\\pm$14\\%). %% registry: plan018 vs plan002 3-seed means\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nThe only ISR seed not dominated by H7 (s42) shows higher raw ISR$^*$ (0.415 vs 0.131), but this is entirely a premise-minimization artifact: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (5.58 vs 8.46 components), biasing LOO upward. %% registry: plan002-additive-b01-s42, plan018-additive-random-b01-s42\nRestricting to non-hacking samples with $\\geq$3 premises: ISR$^*{=}0.258$ ($n{=}11$) vs random ISR$^*{=}0.243$ ($n{=}21$)---a 1.06$\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage. %% D051 premise-count-matched analysis\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): classic Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR causes PR collapse to 18\\% (vs.\\ 48\\% random), completing the Goodhart trajectory~\\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization} at the meta level: low-dose proxy gaming followed by high-dose performance collapse, with the proxy itself a gaming detector. %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42, plan017-additive-random-s42\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\nReplication in Prolog/SWI-Prolog confirms diagnostic transfer (full results: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).\nExec-only: 2/3 seeds show ISR$^*{\\approx}0$ despite moderate-high PR, reflecting \\emph{redundant coding} (repeated ground facts), not absent reasoning. %% registry: plan006-prolog-exec-s42 (ISR*=0.037), plan006-prolog-exec-s43-r3 (ISR*=0.015), plan006-prolog-exec-s44 (ISR*=0.387, small-sample n=21); C006\nISR-as-reward yields 0\\% non-hacking across 185 proved samples via three distinct mechanisms: s42 H7 tautologies (ISR$^*{=}1.0$); s43 uppercase pseudo-Prolog functionally equivalent to H7; s44 query-as-fact gaming that bypasses text classifiers (Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}). %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s42, plan006-prolog-sta-s43, plan006-prolog-sta-s44\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.9\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}\n\\caption{Hacking composition across reward conditions (proved samples). ISR converts H1 (conclusion embedding) into more extreme H7 (conclusion duplication) without reducing total hacking; SFT is near-zero hacking (92.9\\% non-hacking).}\n\\label{fig:hacking-composition}\n\\end{figure}\n\nSeven modes span two logic types (definitions and breakdown in Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}): \\textbf{H1} (GRPO-universal, 68--97\\%), \\textbf{H7} (ISR-specific, 64.5\\% vs.\\ 0\\% random), \\textbf{tautological collapse} (exec-only s44), \\textbf{repetition collapse} (exec-only s43), \\textbf{contradiction explosion} (1000-step), \\textbf{complexity-without-utility} (random $\\beta{=}0.1$), and \\textbf{query-as-fact} (Prolog ISR s44). %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s44, plan001-exec-only-s43-r4, plan016-extended-1000-s42, plan006-prolog-sta-s44\nThe reward signal determines hacking \\emph{composition} but not \\emph{prevalence}: ISR converts H1 into H7 without reducing totals (${\\sim}82$\\% pooled); SFT alone shows near-zero hacking (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}). %% registry: plan009-sft-baseline-s42-r4",
